    12	// SURFACE: driven through the real cli router run(argv, io) — the plan requires

    14	// returns exit 2 ("unknown command 'loop'"); the target-behavior assertions

    16	//
    17	// CONTRACT PINNED HERE:
    18	//   - `baton loop` (no subcommand) -> usage error exit 2 (names the missing
    19	//     subcommand, not "unknown command 'loop'").
    20	//   - unknown subcommand -> exit 2 NAMING it.
    21	//   - `loop init` with no goal -> usage error exit 2 (names 'goal').
    22	//   - `loop init "<goal>"` -> writes loop.json at the resolved root (honors
    23	//     --root) = exactly defaultLoopSpec(goal); prints a summary; exit 0.
    24	//     --json -> one envelope data {created:true, path}.
    25	//   - --dry-run: prints the plan, writes NOTHING (history spy).
    26	//   - idempotent: existing loop.json -> NO overwrite, exit 0, data {created:false},
    27	//     existing bytes untouched.
    28	//   - strict flags: stray positional beyond goal / unknown flag -> exit 2.
    29	//   - the supervised-child guard does NOT apply (loop is supervisor-side):
    30	//     BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD set + `loop init` still writes, exit 0.

    35	/** stdout must be exactly one parseable envelope line. */
    36	function theEnvelope(io) {
    37	  const out = io.stdoutText().trim();
    38	  const lines = out === '' ? [] : out.split('\n');
    39	  assert.equal(lines.length, 1, `expected exactly one stdout line, got: ${JSON.stringify(io.stdoutText())}`);
    40	  const env = JSON.parse(lines[0]);
    41	  for (const k of ['ok', 'data', 'warnings', 'error']) assert.ok(k in env, `envelope carries ${k}`);
    42	  return env;
    43	}

    12	//   validateLoopSpec(spec, config) -> {ok:true, spec} | {ok:false, errors:[strings]}
    13	//
    14	// RED MECHANISM (coordinator guidance): dynamic import with catch, so each test
    15	// reds on a MEANINGFUL assertion ("module must load") rather than a bare
    16	// ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND crash. Once spec.mjs exists these pin its behavior.
    17	//
    18	// CONTRACT PINNED HERE:
    19	//   defaultLoopSpec(goal):
    20	//     - schema 'baton/loop@1'; goal echoed; constraints []; smoke {cmd:null, expect:null}
    21	//     - phases: ordered {id, role} — plan(planner), gate-1(plan-reviewer),
    22	//       smoke-tests(test-author), smoke-verify(test-verifier),
    23	//       smoke-build(implementer), then the subtask cycle roles
    24	//       (test-author, test-verifier, implementer, subtask-reviewer),
    25	//       gate-2 twice (final-reviewer-a, final-reviewer-b). The ROLE SEQUENCE is
    26	//       pinned exactly; the explicitly-named ids are pinned; loosely-named ids
    27	//       (subtask cycle) are only required to be non-empty strings.
    28	//     - budgets {iterationCap:5, perRoleTimeoutMin:30, maxChildrenPerPhase:10}
    29	//   validateLoopSpec: rejections each NAME the offender; ALL problems reported;
    30	//     the 5-cap is a HARD invariant (iterationCap>5 is INVALID); valid spec ->
    31	//     ok with defaults filled (absent budgets -> defaults; absent smoke ->
    32	//     {cmd:null,expect:null}).
    33	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

    38	  mod = await import('../../core/src/loop/spec.mjs');
    39	} catch (e) {
    40	  importError = e;
    41	}
    42	/** Meaningful red until the module exists. */
    43	function M() {
    44	  assert.ok(mod, `core/src/loop/spec.mjs must load (import error: ${importError?.message ?? 'none'})`);

    48	// The default-spec role sequence, fully specified by the plan.
    49	const ROLE_SEQUENCE = [
    50	  'planner',
    51	  'plan-reviewer',
    52	  'test-author',
    53	  'test-verifier',
    54	  'implementer',
    55	  'test-author',
    56	  'test-verifier',

    59	  'final-reviewer-a',
    60	  'final-reviewer-b',
    61	];
    62	
    63	// A config carrying every role the default spec references (the real matrix ids).
    64	function fullConfig() {
    65	  return {
    66	    schema: 'baton/config@1',
    67	    roles: {
    68	      planner: ['claude-code/claude-fable-5'],
    69	      'plan-reviewer': ['codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh'],
    70	      'test-author': ['claude-code/claude-opus-4-8'],
    71	      'test-verifier': ['codex/gpt-5.5@xhigh'],
    72	      implementer: ['claude-code/claude-fable-5'],
    73	      'subtask-reviewer': ['codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh'],
    74	      'final-reviewer-a': ['codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh'],
    75	      'final-reviewer-b': ['claude-code/claude-fable-5@xhigh'],
    76	    },
    77	    platforms: { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} },
    78	    defaults: { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' },
    79	  };
    80	}

    82	/** A minimal, otherwise-valid spec used as the base for single-defect cases. */
    83	function validSpec(over = {}) {
    84	  return {
    85	    schema: 'baton/loop@1',
    86	    goal: 'Ship the widget',
    87	    constraints: [],
    88	    smoke: { cmd: null, expect: null },

    90	      { id: 'plan', role: 'planner' },
    91	      { id: 'gate-1', role: 'plan-reviewer' },
    92	    ],

    94	    ...over,
    95	  };
    96	}

    16	// The ten user-facing commands are the frozen v1 contract; session-start is an
    17	// adapter-facing addition prescribed by gate-2 finding 8 (the cheap
    18	// SessionStart gate Codex/Cursor hooks call — always exit 0).
    19	const COMMANDS = [
    20	  'checkpoint',
    21	  'finalize',

    30	  'session-start',
    31	];
    32	
    33	function version() {
    34	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
    35	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
    36	}
    37	
    38	const USAGE = `usage: baton <command> [options]
    39	
    40	commands:
    41	  ${COMMANDS.join(', ')}
    42	
    43	global options:
    44	  --version   print version
    45	  --json      machine-readable output: one compact JSON envelope on stdout
    46	`;
    47	
    48	/**
    49	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
    50	 * @param {any} io
    51	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
    52	 */
    53	function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
    54	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    55	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    56	}
    57	
    58	/**
    59	 * @param {string[]} argv process args after the binary
    60	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, stdout, stderr}
    61	 * @returns {number | Promise<number>} exit code
    62	 */
    63	export function run(argv, io) {
    64	  const args = argv.filter((a) => a !== '');
    65	  // Global --json intent is parsed BEFORE dispatch (gate-2 iter-2 M6): a
    66	  // top-level `--json` with no command, or --help/--version, must still emit
    67	  // exactly one envelope rather than raw text.
    68	  const jsonWanted = args.includes('--json');
    69	  const cmdIndex = args.findIndex((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
    70	  const cmd = cmdIndex === -1 ? undefined : args[cmdIndex];
    71	
    72	  if (cmd === undefined) {
    73	    // No command — just flags (or nothing).
    74	    if (args.includes('--version')) {
    75	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { version: version() } });
    76	      else io.stdout.write(version() + '\n');
    77	      return 0;
    78	    }
    79	    if (args.includes('--help')) {
    80	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage: USAGE.trim() } });
    81	      else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    82	      return 0;
    83	    }
    84	    // Bare invocation (or a lone --json): a usage error.
    85	    if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'no command given' } });
    86	    else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
    87	    return 2;
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  // All flags (before and after the command token) reach the command; only the
    91	  // command token itself is stripped, so a leading `--json` isn't lost.
    92	  const rest = args.filter((_, i) => i !== cmdIndex);
    93	
    94	  // Usage errors honor the --json envelope contract too (gate-2 fix 11 + M6:
    95	  // global --json counts, wherever it sits).
    96	  const wantsJson = jsonWanted;
    97	  if (cmd === 'wrap') {
    98	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1' } });
    99	    else io.stderr.write(`baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1.\n${USAGE}`);
   100	    return 2;
   101	  }
   102	  if (!COMMANDS.includes(cmd)) {
   103	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: `unknown command '${cmd}'` } });
   104	    else io.stderr.write(`baton: unknown command '${cmd}'\n${USAGE}`);
   105	    return 2;
   106	  }
   107	
   108	  if (rest.includes('--help')) {
   109	    // Subcommand help honors the global --json envelope too (iter-3 F8): with
   110	    // --json it must emit exactly one envelope, never raw usage text.
   111	    const usage = `usage: baton ${cmd} [options]\n\nSee README for the ${cmd} contract.`;
   112	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage } });
   113	    else io.stdout.write(`${usage}\n`);
   114	    return 0;
   115	  }
   116	
   117	  if (cmd === 'status') return cmdStatus(rest, io);
   118	  if (cmd === 'detect') return cmdDetect(rest, io);
   119	  if (cmd === 'remap') return cmdRemap(rest, io);
   120	  if (cmd === 'checkpoint') return cmdCheckpoint(rest, io);
   121	  if (cmd === 'finalize') return cmdFinalize(rest, io);
   122	  if (cmd === 'receive') return cmdReceive(rest, io);
   123	  if (cmd === 'init') return cmdInit(rest, io);
   124	  if (cmd === 'doctor') return cmdDoctor(rest, io);
   125	  if (cmd === 'purge-transcript') return cmdPurgeTranscript(rest, io);
   126	  if (cmd === 'session-start') return cmdSessionStart(rest, io);
   127	  if (cmd === 'recover') return cmdRecover(rest, io);
   128	
   129	  const error = { code: 'not-implemented', msg: `baton ${cmd}: not implemented yet` };
   130	  if (rest.includes('--json')) {
   131	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
   132	  } else {
   133	    io.stderr.write(`${error.msg}\n`);
   134	  }
   135	  return 1;
   136	}

    16	
    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and

    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
    26	   every gate.
    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
    30	
    31	## Design
    32	
    33	### Loop spec
    34	
    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
    37	
    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
    51	  hardcoded models.
    52	
    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
    54	
    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
    59	
    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
    62	  re-check before merge.
    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
    68	
    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
    70	
    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
    77	
    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
    79	
    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
    86	
    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used

     1	import { normSep, joinNorm } from '../util/pathnorm.mjs';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
     5	 * @param {any} io
     6	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
     7	 */
     8	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
     9	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    10	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    11	}
    12	
    13	/**
    14	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
    15	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
    16	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
    17	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    18	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg
    19	 * @returns {number}
    20	 */
    21	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
    22	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
    23	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);
    24	  return 2;
    25	}
    26	
    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}
    68	
    69	/**
    70	 * True when this process is a loop-supervised child (BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD
    71	 * set to any non-empty value). Supervised children must never read or write
    72	 * any bundle and never receive resume context — hook-invoked commands check
    73	 * this BEFORE flag parsing and silently no-op (exit 0, empty output) so the
    74	 * supervisor never has to filter child output or nonzero exits.
    75	 * @param {any} io
    76	 * @returns {boolean}
    77	 */
    78	export function isSupervisedChild(io) {
    79	  const v = io?.env?.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD;
    80	  return typeof v === 'string' && v.length > 0;
    81	}
    82	
    83	/** The supported platform enum, validated wherever a platform id is intake. */
    84	export const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * Validate a platform id against the enum (surface-audit fold): a typo'd
    88	 * platform used to silently seed persistent state whose bogus origin then
    89	 * rejected the REAL platform's checkpoints as foreign.
    90	 * @param {string | boolean | undefined} value
    91	 * @returns {string | null} an error message, or null when valid/absent
    92	 */
    93	export function platformError(value) {
    94	  if (value === undefined) return null;
    95	  if (typeof value === 'string' && PLATFORMS.includes(value)) return null;
    96	  return `platform must be one of ${PLATFORMS.join('|')} (got ${JSON.stringify(value)})`;
    97	}
    98	
    99	/**
   100	 * Strict flag parser (surface-audit fold; docs/design/core.md §tooling always
   101	 * specified strict parsing). spec maps each flag name to 'string' | 'boolean';
   102	 * `json` and `root` are implied common flags. Errors — unknown flag, stray
   103	 * positional, missing string-flag value — return {error} so the command exits
   104	 * 2 instead of silently losing intent: the lenient parser let a stray token
   105	 * flip SAFETY flags off (checkpoint/doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
   106	 * receive --print-prompt all read fail-dangerous exit 0).
   107	 * @param {string[]} args
   108	 * @param {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} spec
   109	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, error?: string}}
   110	 */
   111	export function parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) {
   112	  /** @type {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} */
   113	  const full = { json: 'boolean', root: 'string', ...spec };
   114	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   115	  const flags = {};
   116	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   117	    const a = args[i];
   118	    if (!a.startsWith('--')) return { flags, error: `unexpected argument '${a}' — flags are --name [value]` };
   119	    const key = a.slice(2);
   120	    const kind = full[key];
   121	    if (kind === undefined) return { flags, error: `unknown flag --${key}` };
   122	    if (kind === 'boolean') {
   123	      // A following token that is not a flag would have been this flag's
   124	      // silent "value" under the lenient parser; reject it loudly.
   125	      const next = args[i + 1];
   126	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   127	        return { flags, error: `unexpected token '${next}' after --${key} (a bare flag takes no value)` };
   128	      }
   129	      flags[key] = true;
   130	      continue;
   131	    }
   132	    const next = args[i + 1];
   133	    // A string flag consumes the next token unless it is itself a KNOWN flag
   134	    // (so `--commit --origin x` errors instead of eating '--origin').
   135	    if (next === undefined || (next.startsWith('--') && full[next.slice(2)] !== undefined)) {
   136	      return { flags, error: `--${key} requires a value` };
   137	    }
   138	    flags[key] = next;
   139	    i += 1;
   140	  }
   141	  return { flags };
   142	}
   143	
   144	/**
   145	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
   146	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
   147	 * @param {string[]} args
   148	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
   149	 */
   150	export function parseFlags(args) {
   151	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   152	  const flags = {};
   153	  /** @type {string[]} */
   154	  const positionals = [];
   155	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   156	    const a = args[i];
   157	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
   158	      const key = a.slice(2);
   159	      const next = args[i + 1];
   160	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   161	        flags[key] = next;
   162	        i += 1;
   163	      } else {
   164	        flags[key] = true;
   165	      }
   166	    } else {
   167	      positionals.push(a);
   168	    }
   169	  }
   170	  return { flags, positionals };
   171	}

     7	/**
     8	 * `baton init` — two-phase scaffolder. The plan phase (planInit) is read-only;
     9	 * `--dry-run` stops there. Apply writes each planned action atomically;
    10	 * refusals (malformed settings, corrupt markers) warn and leave their file
    11	 * untouched while the rest of the scaffold still lands.
    12	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    13	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    14	 */
    15	export async function cmdInit(args, io) {
    16	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { codex: 'boolean', cursor: 'boolean', 'with-legacy-prompts': 'boolean', force: 'boolean', check: 'boolean', 'dry-run': 'boolean' });
    17	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'init', parsed.error);
    18	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    19	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    20	
    21	  // The strict parser rejects stray tokens outright (a pasted `# comment` used
    22	  // to be consumed as --dry-run's "value", silently running a REAL init), so
    23	  // boolean flags here are only ever true | undefined.
    24	  const checkMode = flags.check === true;
    25	  const dryRun = flags['dry-run'] === true;
    26	
    27	  /** @type {ReturnType<typeof planInit>} */
    28	  let actions;
    29	  try {
    30	    const base = planInit(root, { force: flags.force === true }, io);
    31	    // Feed the harness writer the gitignore text AS THE BASE SCAFFOLD LEAVES IT
    32	    // (planned write, else current file) so its own ignore lines chain on top —
    33	    // two independent reads would make the later write clobber the earlier one.
    34	    const gi = base.find((a) => a.id === 'gitignore');
    35	    /** @type {string | null} */
    36	    let gitignoreBase = null;
    37	    if (gi?.op === 'write') {
    38	      gitignoreBase = /** @type {string} */ (gi.preview);
    39	    } else {
    40	      try {
    41	        gitignoreBase = io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/.gitignore`, 'utf8');
    42	      } catch {
    43	        gitignoreBase = null;
    44	      }
    45	    }
    46	    actions = [
    47	      ...base,
    48	      ...planHarnessInit(
    49	        root,
    50	        { codex: flags.codex === true, cursor: flags.cursor === true, withLegacyPrompts: flags['with-legacy-prompts'] === true, gitignoreBase },
    51	        io,
    52	      ),
    53	    ];
    54	  } catch (err) {
    55	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    56	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'plan-failed', msg } });
    57	    else io.stderr.write(`baton init: ${msg}\n`);
    58	    return 1;
    59	  }
    60	
    61	  const describe = (/** @type {any} */ a) =>
    62	    a.op === 'write' ? `write ${a.path}` : a.op === 'skip' ? `skip  ${a.path} (${a.note})` : `REFUSE ${a.path} — ${a.note}`;
    63	
    64	  // --check (gate-2 fix 9): the CI drift gate. Read-only — a scaffolded tree
    65	  // where init would change nothing passes; anything init would write or
    66	  // refuse is drift and fails the check.
    67	  if (checkMode) {
    68	    const drift = actions.filter((a) => a.op !== 'skip');
    69	    if (flags.json) {
    70	      emitEnvelope(io, {
    71	        ok: drift.length === 0,
    72	        data: { drift: drift.map(({ id, path, op, note }) => ({ id, path, op, note: note ?? null })) },
    73	      });
    74	    } else if (drift.length === 0) {
    75	      io.stdout.write('baton init --check: no drift — rendered files match template output\n');
    76	    } else {
    77	      io.stderr.write(`baton init --check: drift detected — init would write:\n${drift.map((a) => `  ${describe(a)}`).join('\n')}\n`);
    78	    }
    79	    return drift.length === 0 ? 0 : 1;
    80	  }
    81	
    82	  if (dryRun) {
    83	    if (flags.json) {
    84	      emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { dryRun: true, actions: actions.map(({ id, path, op, note }) => ({ id, path, op, note: note ?? null })) } });
    85	    } else {
    86	      io.stdout.write(`baton init plan (dry run — nothing written):\n${actions.map((a) => `  ${describe(a)}`).join('\n')}\n`);
    87	    }
    88	    return 0;
    89	  }
    90	
    91	  /** @type {string[]} */
    92	  const warnings = [];
    93	  for (const a of actions) {
    94	    if (a.op === 'refuse') {
    95	      warnings.push(a.note ?? `refused ${a.path}`);
    96	      continue;
    97	    }
    98	    if (a.op !== 'write') continue;
    99	    ensureDir(io.fs, dirname(a.path));
   100	    atomicWriteText(io.fs, a.path, /** @type {string} */ (a.preview));
   101	  }
   102	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton init: ${w} — left untouched\n`);
   103	
   104	  if (flags.json) {
   105	    emitEnvelope(io, {
   106	      ok: true,
   107	      data: { actions: actions.map(({ id, path, op, note }) => ({ id, path, op, note: note ?? null })) },
   108	      warnings,
   109	    });
   110	  } else {
   111	    io.stdout.write(`baton init:\n${actions.map((a) => `  ${describe(a)}`).join('\n')}\n`);
   112	  }
   113	  return 0;
   114	}

     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
    35	} = {}) {
    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    37	  const outChunks = [];
    38	  const errChunks = [];
    39	  let currentNow = now;
    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    42	
    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    45	
    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    52	        return reject(e);
    53	      }
    54	      const r = execResults[key];
    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    58	      if (code !== 0) {
    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    61	        e.code = code;
    62	        e.stdout = out;
    63	        e.stderr = err;
    64	        return reject(e);
    65	      }
    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    67	    });
    68	  };
    69	
    70	  return {
    71	    cwd: '/repo',
    72	    env,
    73	    stdin,
    74	    stdout,
    75	    stderr,
    76	    fs,
    77	    execFile,
    78	    now: () => currentNow,
    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    80	    execPath,
    81	    host,
    82	    pid,
    83	    startTime,
    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
    92	  };
    93	}

     1	// In-memory fs fake for unit tests. Synchronous subset only.
     2	// Atomicity contract: renameSync moves content in a single step, so a target
     3	// path is never observed carrying partial content. Every mutation appends a
     4	// full snapshot to `fs.__history`, letting tests enumerate intermediate states
     5	// and prove the tmp+rename discipline (target's first appearance is a rename).
     6	
     7	function fsError(code, syscall, path) {
     8	  const e = new Error(`${code}: ${syscall} '${path}'`);
     9	  e.code = code;
    10	  e.syscall = syscall;
    11	  e.path = path;
    12	  return e;
    13	}
    14	
    15	function normalize(p) {
    16	  const str = String(p);
    17	  const isAbs = str.startsWith('/');
    18	  const out = [];
    19	  for (const part of str.split('/')) {
    20	    if (part === '' || part === '.') continue;
    21	    if (part === '..') {
    22	      out.pop();
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    out.push(part);
    26	  }
    27	  return (isAbs ? '/' : '') + out.join('/');
    28	}
    29	
    30	function dirname(np) {
    31	  const i = np.lastIndexOf('/');
    32	  if (i <= 0) return '/';
    33	  return np.slice(0, i);
    34	}
    35	
    36	function basename(np, parent) {
    37	  return parent === '/' ? np.slice(1) : np.slice(parent.length + 1);
    38	}
    39	
    40	export function makeMemfs(initialFiles = {}) {
    41	  const store = new Map(); // path -> string content
    42	  const dirs = new Set(['/']);
    43	  const mtimes = new Map();
    44	  const history = [];
    45	  let clock = 0;
    46	
    47	  const snapshot = () => Object.fromEntries(store);
    48	  const record = (op, paths) => history.push({ op, paths, files: snapshot() });
    49	  const touch = (np) => mtimes.set(np, ++clock);
    50	
    51	  const mkdirp = (np) => {
    52	    let cur = '';
    53	    for (const part of np.split('/').filter(Boolean)) {
    54	      cur = cur + '/' + part;
    55	      dirs.add(cur);
    56	    }
    57	  };
    58	
    59	  const ensureParent = (np, syscall) => {
    60	    const parent = dirname(np);
    61	    if (parent === '/' ) return;
    62	    if (!dirs.has(parent)) throw fsError('ENOENT', syscall, np);
    63	  };
    64	
    65	  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(initialFiles)) {
    66	    const np = normalize(p);
    67	    mkdirp(dirname(np));
    68	    store.set(np, String(content));
    69	    touch(np);
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  const fs = {
    73	    readFileSync(p /*, enc */) {
    74	      const np = normalize(p);
    75	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'open', np);
    76	      return store.get(np);
    77	    },
    78	
    79	    writeFileSync(p, data /*, enc */) {
    80	      const np = normalize(p);
    81	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    82	      store.set(np, String(data));
    83	      touch(np);
    84	      record('writeFileSync', [np]);
    85	    },
    86	
    87	    appendFileSync(p, data) {
    88	      const np = normalize(p);
    89	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    90	      store.set(np, (store.get(np) || '') + String(data));
    91	      touch(np);
    92	      record('appendFileSync', [np]);
    93	    },
    94	
    95	    renameSync(from, to) {
    96	      const nf = normalize(from);
    97	      const nt = normalize(to);
    98	      if (store.has(nf)) {
    99	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   100	        const content = store.get(nf);
   101	        store.delete(nf);
   102	        store.set(nt, content);
   103	        touch(nt);
   104	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   105	        return;
   106	      }
   107	      // Directory rename (atomic move of the node and all descendants) — the
   108	      // lock's dead-reclaim arbitration renames the stale lock dir aside.
   109	      if (dirs.has(nf)) {
   110	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   111	        const pre = nf + '/';
   112	        dirs.delete(nf);
   113	        dirs.add(nt);
   114	        for (const k of [...store.keys()]) {
   115	          if (k.startsWith(pre)) {
   116	            store.set(nt + k.slice(nf.length), store.get(k));
   117	            store.delete(k);
   118	          }
   119	        }
   120	        for (const d of [...dirs]) {
   121	          if (d !== nt && d.startsWith(pre)) {
   122	            dirs.add(nt + d.slice(nf.length));
   123	            dirs.delete(d);
   124	          }
   125	        }
   126	        touch(nt);
   127	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   128	        return;
   129	      }
   130	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rename', nf);
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    mkdirSync(p, opts = {}) {
   134	      const np = normalize(p);
   135	      if (opts.recursive) {
   136	        mkdirp(np);
   137	        record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   138	        return undefined;
   139	      }
   140	      ensureParent(np, 'mkdir');
   141	      if (dirs.has(np) || store.has(np)) throw fsError('EEXIST', 'mkdir', np);
   142	      dirs.add(np);
   143	      record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   144	      return undefined;
   145	    },
   146	
   147	    existsSync(p) {
   148	      const np = normalize(p);
   149	      return store.has(np) || dirs.has(np);
   150	    },
   151	
   152	    readdirSync(p) {
   153	      const np = normalize(p);
   154	      if (!dirs.has(np)) {
   155	        if (store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOTDIR', 'scandir', np);
   156	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'scandir', np);
   157	      }
   158	      const names = new Set();
   159	      const collect = (k) => {
   160	        if (k !== np && dirname(k) === np) names.add(basename(k, np));
   161	      };
   162	      for (const k of store.keys()) collect(k);
   163	      for (const d of dirs) collect(d);
   164	      return [...names];
   165	    },
   166	
   167	    unlinkSync(p) {
   168	      const np = normalize(p);
   169	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'unlink', np);
   170	      store.delete(np);
   171	      record('unlinkSync', [np]);
   172	    },
   173	
   174	    rmSync(p, opts = {}) {
   175	      const np = normalize(p);
   176	      const isFile = store.has(np);
   177	      const isDir = dirs.has(np);
   178	      if (!isFile && !isDir) {
   179	        if (opts.force) return;
   180	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rm', np);
   181	      }
   182	      if (isFile) store.delete(np);
   183	      if (isDir) {
   184	        const pre = np + '/';
   185	        if (!opts.recursive) {
   186	          const hasChildren = [...store.keys(), ...dirs].some((k) => k !== np && k.startsWith(pre));
   187	          if (hasChildren) throw fsError('ENOTEMPTY', 'rmdir', np);
   188	          dirs.delete(np);
   189	        } else {
   190	          for (const k of [...store.keys()]) if (k === np || k.startsWith(pre)) store.delete(k);
   191	          for (const d of [...dirs]) if (d === np || d.startsWith(pre)) dirs.delete(d);
   192	        }
   193	      }
   194	      record('rmSync', [np]);
   195	    },
   196	
   197	    statSync(p) {
   198	      const np = normalize(p);
   199	      if (store.has(np)) {
   200	        const size = Buffer.byteLength(store.get(np));
   201	        return { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   202	      }
   203	      if (dirs.has(np)) {
   204	        return { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size: 0, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   205	      }
   206	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'stat', np);
   207	    },
   208	
   209	    // The fake cannot represent symlinks, so lstat === stat and realpath is
   210	    // normalization. Symlink-refusal behavior is proven over the real fs in
   211	    // tests/integration/symlink-escape.test.mjs.
   212	    lstatSync(p) {
   213	      return fs.statSync(p);
   214	    },
   215	
   216	    realpathSync(p) {
   217	      const np = normalize(p);
   218	      if (!store.has(np) && !dirs.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'realpath', np);
   219	      return np;
   220	    },
   221	
   222	    copyFileSync(src, dest) {
   223	      const ns = normalize(src);
   224	      const nd = normalize(dest);
   225	      if (!store.has(ns)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'copyfile', ns);
   226	      ensureParent(nd, 'copyfile');
   227	      store.set(nd, store.get(ns));
   228	      touch(nd);
   229	      record('copyFileSync', [ns, nd]);
   230	    },
   231	  };
   232	
   233	  // Introspection hook for atomicity assertions; not part of the real fs API.
   234	  fs.__history = history;
   235	
   236	  return { fs, files: () => snapshot() };
   237	}

    18	/** stdout must be exactly one parseable envelope line. */

     1	/**
     2	 * Load + validate baton.config.json, parsing role chains from their
     3	 * "platform/model[@effort]" string form into structured entries.
     4	 * Never throws: returns {config, errors} with dotted-path errors.
     5	 * @param {string} cwd @param {any} io
     6	 * @returns {{config: any | null, errors: {path: string, msg: string}[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function loadConfig(cwd, io) {
     9	  const file = `${cwd}/baton.config.json`;
    10	  /** @type {{path: string, msg: string}[]} */
    11	  const errors = [];
    12	
    13	  let raw;
    14	  try {
    15	    raw = io.fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8');
    16	  } catch {
    17	    return { config: null, errors: [{ path: '', msg: `baton.config.json not found at ${file} — run 'baton init' to scaffold it` }] };
    18	  }
    19	  let parsed;
    20	  try {
    21	    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    22	  } catch (err) {
    23	    return { config: null, errors: [{ path: '', msg: `baton.config.json is not valid JSON (${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? 'parse error'})` }] };
    24	  }
    25	
    26	  if (parsed.roles === null || typeof parsed.roles !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed.roles)) {
    27	    errors.push({ path: 'roles', msg: 'roles must be an object mapping role names to chain arrays' });
    28	  }
    29	
    30	  /** @type {Record<string, any[]>} */
    31	  const roles = {};
    32	  if (errors.length === 0) {
    33	    for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(parsed.roles)) {
    34	      if (!Array.isArray(chain)) {
    35	        errors.push({ path: `roles.${role}`, msg: 'a role chain must be an array of "platform/model[@effort]" strings' });
    36	        continue;
    37	      }
    38	      /** @type {any[]} */
    39	      const entries = [];
    40	      for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    41	        const s = chain[i];
    42	        const slash = typeof s === 'string' ? s.indexOf('/') : -1;
    43	        if (slash <= 0 || slash === s.length - 1) {
    44	          errors.push({ path: `roles.${role}[${i}]`, msg: `malformed chain entry ${JSON.stringify(s)} — expected "platform/model[@effort]"` });
    45	          continue;
    46	        }
    47	        const platform = s.slice(0, slash);
    48	        const rest = s.slice(slash + 1);
    49	        const at = rest.indexOf('@');
    50	        const model = at === -1 ? rest : rest.slice(0, at);
    51	        const effort = at === -1 ? null : rest.slice(at + 1);
    52	        if (parsed.platforms && !(platform in parsed.platforms)) {
    53	          errors.push({ path: `roles.${role}[${i}]`, msg: `unknown platform "${platform}" — not a key of config.platforms` });
    54	          continue;
    55	        }
    56	        entries.push({ platform, model, effort });
    57	      }
    58	      roles[role] = entries;
    59	    }
    60	  }
    61	
    62	  if (errors.length > 0) return { config: null, errors };
    63	  return { config: { ...parsed, roles }, errors };
    64	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Pure, explainable role resolution over the committed matrix. Every decision
     3	 * is auditable: chosen entries carry their chainIndex; passed-over entries land
     4	 * in skipped[] with a why. avoid[] population is the caller's job.
     5	 * @param {{config: any, to: string, avoid?: string[], avoidEntries?: Array<{platform: string, model: string}>, nativeOnly?: boolean, probes?: Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}} input
     6	 * @returns {{assignments: Record<string, any>, notes: string[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function resolveRoles({ config, to, avoid = [], avoidEntries = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null }) {
     9	  /** @type {string[]} */
    10	  const notes = [];
    11	
    12	  /**
    13	   * The eligibility verdict for a platform, or null when eligible.
    14	   * @param {string} platform @returns {string | null}
    15	   */
    16	  function skipReason(platform) {
    17	    if (avoid.includes(platform)) return 'avoided';
    18	    if (!(platform in (config.platforms ?? {}))) return 'unknown-platform';
    19	    if (config.platforms[platform]?.enabled === false) return 'disabled';
    20	    if (probes && probes[platform]) {
    21	      const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    22	      if (outcome === 'rate-limited') return 'rate-limited';
    23	      if (capability === 'not-installed') return 'not-installed';
    24	      // 'installed' means installation was PROVEN but the probe was capped there
    25	      // (capOnSuccess) — authentication is UNVERIFIED, not verified-failed. Only
    26	      // a probe that ran and did NOT succeed (outcome !== 'ok') is a verified
    27	      // auth/reachability failure worth skipping; a capped success stays
    28	      // selectable and is flagged degraded below (iter-3 F1, plan §Role matrix).
    29	      if (capability === 'installed' && outcome !== 'ok') return 'unauthenticated';
    30	    }
    31	    return null;
    32	  }
    33	
    34	  // A selection is degraded when its availability is UNVERIFIED (gate-2 iter-2
    35	  // M3, iter-3 F1): with no probe cache at all, resolution is offline and EVERY
    36	  // selection is degraded (plan: "Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged
    37	  // degraded"); with a partial cache, platforms the cache is missing are; and a
    38	  // platform probed only to 'installed' (auth unverified, e.g. a --version
    39	  // success) is degraded even though it stays selectable.
    40	  /** @param {string} platform */
    41	  const probeUnverified = (platform) => {
    42	    if (probes == null || !probes[platform]) return true;
    43	    const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    44	    // Degraded when ANY required dimension is unverified: a capped 'installed'
    45	    // (auth never confirmed) OR a non-ok outcome (iter-4 I6) — a selectable
    46	    // platform whose last probe ERRORED or TIMED OUT is presented as
    47	    // reachability-verified otherwise, which is exactly the silent-degradation
    48	    // the plan's degraded flag exists to surface. (rate-limited is skipped, not
    49	    // selected, so it never reaches here.)
    50	    return capability === 'installed' || outcome !== 'ok';
    51	  };
    52	
    53	  // Entry-level avoidance (plan §Model-level failover): a specific
    54	  // {platform, model} tuple is dead (e.g. account-tier rejected) while its
    55	  // platform stays usable. Matching is on model IDENTITY — an @effort suffix
    56	  // on either side is ignored — and requires BOTH dimensions, so the same
    57	  // model name on another platform is never caught.
    58	  /** @param {any} m */
    59	  const baseModel = (m) => (typeof m === 'string' ? m.split('@')[0] : m);
    60	  /** @param {string} platform @param {string} model */
    61	  const entryAvoided = (platform, model) =>
    62	    avoidEntries.some((a) => a?.platform === platform && baseModel(a?.model) === baseModel(model));
    63	
    64	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
    65	  const assignments = {};
    66	  for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(config.roles)) {
    67	    /** @type {any[]} */
    68	    const skipped = [];
    69	    /** @type {any} */
    70	    let assignment = null;
    71	
    72	    for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    73	      const entry = chain[i];
    74	      if (nativeOnly && entry.platform !== to) {
    75	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'non-native' });
    76	        continue;
    77	      }
    78	      const why = skipReason(entry.platform);
    79	      if (why) {
    80	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why });
    81	        continue;
    82	      }
    83	      // After skipReason so a dual match keeps the platform-level label.
    84	      if (entryAvoided(entry.platform, entry.model)) {
    85	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'avoided-entry' });
    86	        continue;
    87	      }
    88	      assignment = {
    89	        platform: entry.platform,
    90	        model: entry.model,
    91	        effort: entry.effort ?? null,
    92	        mode: entry.platform === to ? 'native' : 'delegated',
    93	        chainIndex: i,
    94	        skipped,
    95	      };
    96	      if (probeUnverified(entry.platform)) assignment.degraded = true;
    97	      break;
    98	    }
    99	
   100	    if (!assignment && nativeOnly) {
   101	      // Forced-default is only valid when the destination itself is eligible.
   102	      const whyTo = skipReason(to);
   103	      const model = config.defaults?.[to];
   104	      if (!whyTo && typeof model === 'string') {
   105	        assignment = { platform: to, model, effort: null, mode: 'forced-default', chainIndex: null, skipped };
   106	        if (probeUnverified(to)) assignment.degraded = true;
   107	      }
   108	    }
   109	
   110	    if (!assignment) {
   111	      assignment = { platform: null, model: null, effort: null, mode: 'unavailable', chainIndex: null, skipped };
   112	    }
   113	    assignments[role] = assignment;
   114	  }
   115	
   116	  return { assignments, notes };
   117	}

docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:7:Saksham's AI-coding workflow spans three harnesses — Claude Code, OpenAI Codex CLI, and Cursor — and breaks whenever one platform's usage limits run out mid-task. Work state, plan context, and the writer/reviewer role assignments are trapped in the dying session. His handwritten notes describe a three-layer system (ideal-loop creator with smoke-test gate; dual-worktree adversarial write/review pipeline; cross-platform workflow portability). **Milestone 1 (this plan): the cross-platform failover layer** — chosen by Saksham as the foundation, since it addresses the stated pain point and the other layers run on top of it.
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:26:   - Overall: a goal with genuine constraints, looped until the constraints are met. Review artifacts under `reviews/<task-id>/` with verdicts `{APPROVED, APPROVED_WITH_NOTES, BLOCKED}` (pattern from Saksham's wt-* repos).
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:56:**Bundle** (`.handoff/`): single active bundle = `bundle.json` (atomic tmp+rename snapshot, `.bak` of previous good) + `journal.ndjson` (append-only events, torn-tail tolerant) + rendered `HANDOFF.md` + `history/` (unified retention: last 10 entries **per type** — finalize freezes, takeover archives, receive archives — each = frozen snapshot plus rotated journal; receipts needed for replay/audit are always preserved within retained entries). Load = snapshot + replay journal entries past `journalSeq`; recovery paths: `.bak` + replay, or full journal rebuild. Schema `baton/bundle@1`: origin platform/model, task goal/constraints/acceptance, plan steps with status, capped decisions log, files touched, role assignments, git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, handoff finalize block (`status: open|sealed|received` + `receive_log[]`), compaction markers. Size discipline is **deterministic truncation only** (decisions capped first-20+last-150 with marker; no LLM summarization).
docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:60:**Journal rotation & crash recovery** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): on every finalize, crash-safe order = write a rotation marker → write frozen snapshot to `history/` → rename `journal.ndjson` alongside it → start a fresh journal seeded with the snapshot's `journalSeq` → clear marker. On load, a present marker deterministically rolls the rotation forward (if the snapshot landed) or back (if not); the same marker pattern governs interrupted forced takeovers and interrupted purges (purge resumes from its marker until the whole tree is clean). Retention: last 10 rotations pruned with the history. Doctor warns at journal > 5 MB; perf test: 10k-event replay < 1 s. **Filesystem support boundary: v1 supports local filesystems only** — lock and rename atomicity are not guaranteed on network/shared mounts (NFS/SMB/cloud-sync folders); doctor detects and warns, README states it.

docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:147:When only one vendor is reachable, test-author/verifier fall back to different models in fresh sessions and every review artifact header carries `degraded: single-vendor` — weakening is auditable, never silent. Review artifacts extend the wt-* convention exactly: `reviews/<task-id>/{gate-1-plan, subtask-NN-tests, gate-2-final/{reviewer-a, reviewer-b, findings.md}}/iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}`, templates ported from `wt-supervisor/reviews/_templates/`. The loop: plan doc in `docs/plans/` with goal + genuine constraints (falsifiable acceptance criteria) → Gate 1 → per-subtask test-first with the implementer forbidden from weakening/deleting tests (modifications re-enter verification) → dual independent fresh-context final reviews → collate `findings.md` → fix blocking items, re-review by the raising reviewer → loop until acceptance constraints are met with evidence attached. **Iteration budget (Saksham's rule): every review gate loops at most 5 times — hard stop after the 5th iteration; on exhaustion, the gate ends with the outstanding findings escalated to Saksham for a human decision instead of a 6th run.** This cap is encoded in the AGENTS.md template's review-gates section and applies to building this repo too.

docs/design/core.md:56:`bundle.json` fields: `schema`, `bundleId`, `generation`, `createdAt/updatedAt`, `origin {platform, model, sessionHint, unstable}`, `task {goal, constraints[], acceptance[]}`, `plan.steps[] {id, title, status: pending|active|done|blocked, note}`, `decisions[] {seq, ts, summary, detail(capped)}`, `files {touched[] {path, op, lastTs}, truncated}`, `roles.assignments`, `git {branch, headSha, dirty, dirtySummary[], contentDigest, summaryTruncated} | null`, `handoff {status: open|sealed|received, reason, reasonClass, toPlatformHint, finalizedAt, receive_log[]}`, `journalSeq`, `compaction {droppedDecisions, note}`, `dedupeRing: string[]` (merge idempotency ring, capped 500, oldest evicted), transcript field (opt-in, redacted).
docs/design/core.md:58:`journal.ndjson` entry: `{seq, ts, type, dedupeKey, writerId, source, payload}`. Types: `decision | plan.set | plan.step | file.touch | note | roles.remap | task.update | git.update`. Every type is self-applicable for replay over `emptyBundle()`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
   140	
   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   142	
   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the
   157	  journal and continues or parks; no orphaned children survive (process
   158	  groups reaped on adoption).
   159	
   160	- **Smoke gate (staleness-proof — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 8; TDD order —
   161	  iteration 2, finding 2)**: after gate-1, the smoke slice is built as a TDD
   162	  mini-cycle (test-author writes the failing smoke assertion → test-verifier
   163	  approves it → implementer writes only enough to pass it — the repo TDD
   164	  contract applies to the slice, not just to subtasks); the supervisor then
   165	  runs `smoke.cmd`, records output, and STOPS, writing
   166	  `.handoff/loop/SMOKE-REVIEW.md` plus `smoke-approval.json` containing an
   167	  approval token = digest over {loop-state digest, smoke.cmd, output digest,
   168	  HEAD, content-sensitive git digest (existing snapshot machinery), child
   169	  assignment}. Resuming requires `baton loop run --approve-smoke <token>`;
   170	  the supervisor recomputes the digest first and REFUSES a drifted token
   171	  (anything changed since the human looked) — it parks for a fresh smoke run
   172	  instead. No full-run budget is spent before a valid approval.
   173	- **Limit failover (the baton integration — Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 3)**:
   174	  an exact transaction, not a loose chain. On child death: (1) reap the
   175	  process group and freeze the transcript; (2) classify via `baton detect`;
   176	  non-limit → the retry/park path above. On a limit classification:
   177	  (3) checkpoint the loop position INTO THE BUNDLE (supervisor
   178	  `checkpoint --session loop-<runId>`) so the seal carries it and the receipt
   179	  token binds it; (4) seal via `finalize --reason-class usage-limit --root
   180	  <mainRoot>` (an already-dead/unsealed bundle takes the degraded-open path

    79	    36	function theEnvelope(io) {
    80	    37	  const out = io.stdoutText().trim();
    81	    38	  const lines = out === '' ? [] : out.split('\n');
    82	    39	  assert.equal(lines.length, 1, `expected exactly one stdout line, got: ${JSON.stringify(io.stdoutText())}`);
    83	    40	  const env = JSON.parse(lines[0]);
    84	    41	  for (const k of ['ok', 'data', 'warnings', 'error']) assert.ok(k in env, `envelope carries ${k}`);
    85	    42	  return env;
    86	    43	}

  2104	    33	function version() {
  2105	    34	  const pkgPath = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'package.json');
  2106	    35	  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8')).version;
  2107	    36	}
  2108	    37	
  2109	    38	const USAGE = `usage: baton <command> [options]
  2110	    39	
  2111	    40	commands:
  2112	    41	  ${COMMANDS.join(', ')}
  2113	    42	
  2114	    43	global options:
  2115	    44	  --version   print version
  2116	    45	  --json      machine-readable output: one compact JSON envelope on stdout
  2117	    46	`;
  2118	    47	
  2119	    48	/**
  2120	    49	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
  2121	    50	 * @param {any} io
  2122	    51	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
  2123	    52	 */
  2124	    53	function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
  2125	    54	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
  2126	    55	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
  2127	    56	}
  2128	    57	
  2129	    58	/**
  2130	    59	 * @param {string[]} argv process args after the binary
  2131	    60	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, stdout, stderr}
  2132	    61	 * @returns {number | Promise<number>} exit code
  2133	    62	 */
  2134	    63	export function run(argv, io) {
  2135	    64	  const args = argv.filter((a) => a !== '');
  2136	    65	  // Global --json intent is parsed BEFORE dispatch (gate-2 iter-2 M6): a
  2137	    66	  // top-level `--json` with no command, or --help/--version, must still emit
  2138	    67	  // exactly one envelope rather than raw text.
  2139	    68	  const jsonWanted = args.includes('--json');
  2140	    69	  const cmdIndex = args.findIndex((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
  2141	    70	  const cmd = cmdIndex === -1 ? undefined : args[cmdIndex];
  2142	    71	
  2143	    72	  if (cmd === undefined) {
  2144	    73	    // No command — just flags (or nothing).
  2145	    74	    if (args.includes('--version')) {
  2146	    75	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { version: version() } });
  2147	    76	      else io.stdout.write(version() + '\n');
  2148	    77	      return 0;
  2149	    78	    }
  2150	    79	    if (args.includes('--help')) {
  2151	    80	      if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage: USAGE.trim() } });
  2152	    81	      else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
  2153	    82	      return 0;
  2154	    83	    }
  2155	    84	    // Bare invocation (or a lone --json): a usage error.
  2156	    85	    if (jsonWanted) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'no command given' } });
  2157	    86	    else io.stdout.write(USAGE);
  2158	    87	    return 2;
  2159	    88	  }
  2160	    89	
  2161	    90	  // All flags (before and after the command token) reach the command; only the
  2162	    91	  // command token itself is stripped, so a leading `--json` isn't lost.
  2163	    92	  const rest = args.filter((_, i) => i !== cmdIndex);
  2164	    93	
  2165	    94	  // Usage errors honor the --json envelope contract too (gate-2 fix 11 + M6:
  2166	    95	  // global --json counts, wherever it sits).
  2167	    96	  const wantsJson = jsonWanted;
  2168	    97	  if (cmd === 'wrap') {
  2169	    98	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: 'baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1' } });
  2170	    99	    else io.stderr.write(`baton wrap is reserved for a future release (PTY supervisor); not available in v1.\n${USAGE}`);
  2171	   100	    return 2;
  2172	   101	  }
  2173	   102	  if (!COMMANDS.includes(cmd)) {
  2174	   103	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg: `unknown command '${cmd}'` } });
  2175	   104	    else io.stderr.write(`baton: unknown command '${cmd}'\n${USAGE}`);
  2176	   105	    return 2;
  2177	   106	  }
  2178	   107	
  2179	   108	  if (rest.includes('--help')) {
  2180	   109	    // Subcommand help honors the global --json envelope too (iter-3 F8): with
  2181	   110	    // --json it must emit exactly one envelope, never raw usage text.
  2182	   111	    const usage = `usage: baton ${cmd} [options]\n\nSee README for the ${cmd} contract.`;
  2183	   112	    if (wantsJson) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { usage } });
  2184	   113	    else io.stdout.write(`${usage}\n`);
  2185	   114	    return 0;
  2186	   115	  }
  2187	   116	
  2188	   117	  if (cmd === 'status') return cmdStatus(rest, io);
  2189	   118	  if (cmd === 'detect') return cmdDetect(rest, io);
  2190	   119	  if (cmd === 'remap') return cmdRemap(rest, io);
  2191	   120	  if (cmd === 'checkpoint') return cmdCheckpoint(rest, io);
  2192	   121	  if (cmd === 'finalize') return cmdFinalize(rest, io);
  2193	   122	  if (cmd === 'receive') return cmdReceive(rest, io);
  2194	   123	  if (cmd === 'init') return cmdInit(rest, io);
  2195	   124	  if (cmd === 'doctor') return cmdDoctor(rest, io);
  2196	   125	  if (cmd === 'purge-transcript') return cmdPurgeTranscript(rest, io);
  2197	   126	  if (cmd === 'session-start') return cmdSessionStart(rest, io);
  2198	   127	  if (cmd === 'recover') return cmdRecover(rest, io);
  2199	   128	
  2200	   129	  const error = { code: 'not-implemented', msg: `baton ${cmd}: not implemented yet` };
  2201	   130	  if (rest.includes('--json')) {
  2202	   131	    emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error });
  2203	   132	  } else {

  2205	   134	  }

  2220	    11	development methodology itself on top of that foundation: a goal-driven loop
  2221	    12	with review gates (Layer 2) and a dual-worktree adversarial write/review

  2223	    14	
  2224	    15	## Locked decisions (Saksham, 2026-07-18)
  2225	    16	
  2226	    17	1. **Packaging**: inside the baton repo — new commands `baton loop` and
  2227	    18	   `baton pipeline`, sharing the role matrix, resolver, doctor, and
  2228	    19	   checkpoint/handoff machinery.
  2229	    20	2. **Layer 3 swap cadence**: per subtask — writer and reviewer swap seats
  2230	    21	   every subtask; the planner role plans and the merger role performs the
  2231	    22	   final adversarial merge (concrete chains live in `baton.config.json`).
  2232	    23	3. **Layer 2 smoke gate**: one staged gate after planning — the loop must
  2233	    24	   produce a minimal end-to-end slice (build + run + one real assertion) and
  2234	    25	   get explicit approval before the full run; the 5-iteration cap governs
  2235	    26	   every gate.
  2236	    27	4. **Driver**: a supervisor CLI — spawns headless role children
  2237	    28	   (`claude -p` / `codex exec`), parses verdicts, and invokes baton handoff
  2238	    29	   automatically on limit death. Survives the operator's session ending.
  2239	    30	
  2240	    31	## Design
  2241	    32	
  2242	    33	### Loop spec
  2243	    34	
  2244	    35	`loop.json` at repo root (schema `baton/loop@1`), scaffolded by
  2245	    36	`baton loop init "<goal>"`:
  2246	    37	
  2247	    38	- `goal`, `constraints[]` (falsifiable acceptance criteria — the loop's exit
  2248	    39	  condition), `smoke` ({cmd, expect} — the staged-gate slice definition),
  2249	    40	- `phases` — an ordered list of `{id, role}` entries: `id` is a free label
  2250	    41	  for progress display; `role` MUST be a matrix role ID (validation fails on
  2251	    42	  any unknown role before a child spawns). Default:
  2252	    43	  plan(`planner`) → gate-1(`plan-reviewer`) → smoke — itself a TDD mini-cycle
  2253	    44	  per the repo contract: smoke-tests(`test-author`) →
  2254	    45	  smoke-verify(`test-verifier`) → smoke-build(`implementer`) → the human
  2255	    46	  smoke gate — → per-subtask cycle (`test-author` → `test-verifier` →
  2256	    47	  `implementer` → `subtask-reviewer`) → gate-2(`final-reviewer-a`,
  2257	    48	  `final-reviewer-b`) → done,
  2258	    49	- `budgets` ({iterationCap: 5, perRoleTimeoutMin, maxChildrenPerPhase}),
  2259	    50	- roles resolve through the existing `baton.config.json` matrix — never
  2260	    51	  hardcoded models.
  2261	    52	
  2262	    53	### Role-matrix additions (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 1)
  2263	    54	
  2264	    55	The loop references roles by ID only; every referenced role must exist in the
  2265	    56	matrix or `loop.json` validation fails (`unknown role '<id>'`, exit 2, before
  2266	    57	any child spawns). Four roles are ADDED to `baton.config.json` +
  2267	    58	`templates/baton.config.json.tpl` in the first implementation task:
  2268	    59	
  2269	    60	- `subtask-reviewer` — per-subtask diff review inside the loop cycle.
  2270	    61	- `merger` — the only role that writes main in pipeline mode; adversarial
  2271	    62	  re-check before merge.
  2272	    63	- `worker-a`, `worker-b` — the two pipeline seats. Subtask N assigns
  2273	    64	  writer = seat (N mod 2), reviewer = the other seat; the SEATS swap, and each
  2274	    65	  seat's concrete platform/model comes from its configured chain via the
  2275	    66	  resolver. No vendor or model name appears in this plan, child prompts, or
  2276	    67	  loop code — chains live in config only.
  2277	    68	
  2278	    69	### `baton loop` (Layer 2)
  2279	    70	
  2280	    71	- `baton loop init <goal>` — scaffold spec + plan template; idempotent,
  2281	    72	  dry-run-able, same two-phase plan/apply discipline as `baton init`.
  2282	    73	- `baton loop run [--approve-smoke <token>]` — the state machine. State lives
  2283	    74	  in gitignored `.handoff/loop/state.json` + `journal.ndjson` (same atomic
  2284	    75	  write + journal replay machinery as the bundle; loop position is also
  2285	    76	  checkpointed into the handoff bundle so a limit death carries it).
  2286	    77	
  2287	    78	### Root + ownership discipline (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 2)
  2288	    79	
  2289	    80	One authoritative root: the MAIN repo root where `loop.json` lives. The
  2290	    81	supervisor is the SOLE writer of `.handoff/loop/` and of the main root's
  2291	    82	handoff bundle; every supervisor-driven baton invocation passes explicit
  2292	    83	`--root <mainRoot>` and a supervisor-owned stable session hint
  2293	    84	(`--session loop-<runId>` — a NEW flag added to `checkpoint`; `receive`
  2294	    85	already has it), so the bundle has exactly one stable owner.
  2295	    86	
  2296	    87	**Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
  2297	    88	finding 1)** — children never touch OR read any bundle, by construction: the
  2298	    89	supervisor sets `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD=1` in every child's environment, and
  2299	    90	EVERY baton hook command — the hook entrypoints, `baton checkpoint`, and
  2300	    91	`baton session-start` (which would otherwise read the bundle and inject
  2301	    92	pending-handoff resume context into the child) — quietly no-ops (exit 0,
  2302	    93	empty output) when it is set. This covers
  2303	    94	Layer-2 children running in the MAIN root (where root discovery would
  2304	    95	otherwise find the supervisor-owned bundle and the foreign-session guard
  2305	    96	would reject their stable hints) and pipeline children in worktrees alike;
  2306	    97	narrative checkpointing for supervised work is the supervisor's job, driven
  2307	    98	by parsed child results. Contract tests: (a) Layer-2 — two concurrent
  2308	    99	main-root children with distinct stable session hints and live hooks →
  2309	   100	zero bundle mutations, zero foreign-session rejections, supervisor
  2310	   101	`--session` checkpoints land; (b) pipeline — same from two worktrees while
  2311	   102	the supervisor writes loop state.
  2312	   103	- Each role invocation is a headless child under the supervisor:
  2313	   104	  - claude-code roles: `claude -p "<role prompt>" --permission-mode
  2314	   105	    acceptEdits --allowedTools ...` (allowlist per role: reviewers read-only).
  2315	   106	  - codex roles: `codex exec -C <root> -s <sandbox> --model <m> -c
  2316	   107	    model_reasoning_effort=<e> "<prompt>" </dev/null` (stdin closed — the
  2317	   108	    stdin-hang class is a known field failure).
  2318	   109	  - Verdict contract: children end with the structured tail
  2319	   110	    (`VERDICT: APPROVED|APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|BLOCKED` + FINDINGS) already used

  2324	     1	import { normSep, joinNorm } from '../util/pathnorm.mjs';
  2325	     2	
  2326	     3	/**
  2327	     4	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
  2328	     5	 * @param {any} io
  2329	     6	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
  2330	     7	 */
  2331	     8	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
  2332	     9	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
  2333	    10	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
  2334	    11	}

  2336	    13	/**
  2337	    14	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
  2338	    15	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
  2339	    16	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
  2340	    17	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
  2341	    18	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg
  2342	    19	 * @returns {number}
  2343	    20	 */
  2344	    21	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
  2345	    22	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
  2346	    23	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);
  2347	    24	  return 2;

  2349	    26	
  2350	    27	/**
  2351	    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
  2352	    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
  2353	    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
  2354	    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
  2355	    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
  2356	    33	 * @returns {string}
  2357	    34	 */
  2358	    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
  2359	    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
  2360	    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
  2361	    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
  2362	    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
  2363	    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
  2364	    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
  2365	    42	  let dir = start;
  2366	    43	  while (true) {
  2367	    44	    try {
  2368	    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
  2369	    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
  2370	    47	    } catch {
  2371	    48	      break;
  2372	    49	    }
  2373	    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
  2374	    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
  2375	    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
  2376	    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
  2377	    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
  2378	    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
  2379	    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
  2380	    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
  2381	    58	        dir = driveRoot;
  2382	    59	        continue;
  2383	    60	      }
  2384	    61	      break;
  2385	    62	    }
  2386	    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
  2387	    64	    dir = parent;
  2388	    65	  }
  2389	    66	  return start;
  2390	    67	}
  2391	    68	
  2392	    69	/**
  2393	    70	 * True when this process is a loop-supervised child (BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD
  2394	    71	 * set to any non-empty value). Supervised children must never read or write
  2395	    72	 * any bundle and never receive resume context — hook-invoked commands check
  2396	    73	 * this BEFORE flag parsing and silently no-op (exit 0, empty output) so the
  2397	    74	 * supervisor never has to filter child output or nonzero exits.
  2398	    75	 * @param {any} io
  2399	    76	 * @returns {boolean}
  2400	    77	 */
  2401	    78	export function isSupervisedChild(io) {
  2402	    79	  const v = io?.env?.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD;
  2403	    80	  return typeof v === 'string' && v.length > 0;
  2404	    81	}
